To implement the task, we will create a Python-based CLI network ping tool with a graphical user interface (GUI). The GUI will allow users to input an IP address or hostname, specify the number of ICMP echo requests, and view the results (round-trip times and summary). We will use the `tkinter` library for the GUI and the `subprocess` module to handle the ping functionality.

### Core Classes and Functions
1. **`PingTool` (Class)**:
   - Purpose: Encapsulates the logic for sending ICMP echo requests and calculating round-trip times.
   - Methods:
     - `ping(host, count)`: Sends ICMP echo requests to the specified host and returns the results.

2. **`PingGUI` (Class)**:
   - Purpose: Manages the graphical user interface for the ping tool.
   - Methods:
     - `__init__(self)`: Initializes the GUI components.
     - `start_ping(self)`: Handles the ping operation when the user clicks the "Ping" button.
     - `display_results(self, results)`: Displays the ping results in the GUI.

3. **Helper Functions**:
   - `parse_ping_output(output)`: Parses the output of the ping command to extract round-trip times and summary.

---

### File 1: `main.py`
This is the main file that initializes and runs the application.

```python
'''

This is the main file for the CLI network ping tool with a GUI. It initializes and runs the application.

'''

from ping_tool import PingTool
from ping_gui import PingGUI

if __name__ == "__main__":
    app = PingGUI()
    app.run()
```

---

### File 2: `ping_tool.py`
This file contains the `PingTool` class, which handles the ping logic.

```python
'''

This file contains the PingTool class, which handles the logic for sending ICMP echo requests and calculating round-trip times.

'''

import subprocess

class PingTool:
    def __init__(self):
        pass

    def ping(self, host, count):
        '''
        Sends ICMP echo requests to the specified host and returns the results.

        :param host: The IP address or hostname to ping.
        :param count: The number of ICMP echo requests to send.
        :return: A tuple containing the raw output and parsed results.
        '''
        try:
            # Execute the ping command
            output = subprocess.check_output(
                ["ping", "-c", str(count), host],
                stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
                universal_newlines=True
            )
            return output
        except subprocess.CalledProcessError as e:
            return f"Error: {e.output}"
```

---

### File 3: `ping_gui.py`
This file contains the `PingGUI` class, which manages the graphical user interface.

```python
'''

This file contains the PingGUI class, which manages the graphical user interface for the ping tool.

'''

import tkinter as tk
from tkinter import messagebox
from ping_tool import PingTool

class PingGUI:
    def __init__(self):
        '''
        Initializes the GUI components.
        '''
        self.ping_tool = PingTool()
        self.root = tk.Tk()
        self.root.title("Ping Tool")

        # Input fields
        self.host_label = tk.Label(self.root, text="Host:")
        self.host_label.grid(row=0, column=0, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.host_entry = tk.Entry(self.root)
        self.host_entry.grid(row=0, column=1, padx=5, pady=5)

        self.count_label = tk.Label(self.root, text="Count:")
        self.count_label.grid(row=1, column=0, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.count_entry = tk.Entry(self.root)
        self.count_entry.grid(row=1, column=1, padx=5, pady=5)

        # Ping button
        self.ping_button = tk.Button(self.root, text="Ping", command=self.start_ping)
        self.ping_button.grid(row=2, column=0, columnspan=2, pady=10)

        # Results display
        self.results_text = tk.Text(self.root, height=15, width=50)
        self.results_text.grid(row=3, column=0, columnspan=2, padx=5, pady=5)

    def start_ping(self):
        '''
        Handles the ping operation when the user clicks the "Ping" button.
        '''
        host = self.host_entry.get()
        count = self.count_entry.get()

        if not host or not count.isdigit():
            messagebox.showerror("Error", "Please enter a valid host and count.")
            return

        count = int(count)
        self.results_text.delete(1.0, tk.END)

        # Perform the ping operation
        results = self.ping_tool.ping(host, count)
        self.display_results(results)

    def display_results(self, results):
        '''
        Displays the ping results in the GUI.

        :param results: The results of the ping operation.
        '''
        self.results_text.insert(tk.END, results)

    def run(self):
        '''
        Runs the GUI application.
        '''
        self.root.mainloop()
```

---

### Summary
- The `main.py` file initializes and runs the application.
- The `ping_tool.py` file contains the logic for sending ICMP echo requests.
- The `ping_gui.py` file manages the graphical user interface.

This implementation provides a functional CLI network ping tool with a user-friendly GUI. The user can input a hostname or IP address, specify the number of ICMP echo requests, and view the results in the GUI.

### File 2: `ping_tool.py`

```python
'''

This file contains the PingTool class, which handles the logic for sending ICMP echo requests and calculating round-trip times.

'''

import subprocess

class PingTool:
    def __init__(self):
        pass

    def ping(self, host, count):
        '''
        Sends ICMP echo requests to the specified host and returns the results.

        :param host: The IP address or hostname to ping.
        :param count: The number of ICMP echo requests to send.
        :return: A tuple containing the raw output and parsed results.
        '''
        try:
            # Execute the ping command
            output = subprocess.check_output(
                ["ping", "-c", str(count), host],
                stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
                universal_newlines=True
            )
            return output
        except subprocess.CalledProcessError as e:
            return f"Error: {e.output}"
```

### File 3: `ping_gui.py`
This file contains the `PingGUI` class, which manages the graphical user interface.

```python
'''

This file contains the PingGUI class, which manages the graphical user interface for the ping tool.

'''

import tkinter as tk
from tkinter import messagebox
from ping_tool import PingTool

class PingGUI:
    def __init__(self):
        '''
        Initializes the GUI components.
        '''
        self.ping_tool = PingTool()
        self.root = tk.Tk()
        self.root.title("Ping Tool")

        # Input fields
        self.host_label = tk.Label(self.root, text="Host:")
        self.host_label.grid(row=0, column=0, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.host_entry = tk.Entry(self.root)
        self.host_entry.grid(row=0, column=1, padx=5, pady=5)

        self.count_label = tk.Label(self.root, text="Count:")
        self.count_label.grid(row=1, column=0, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.count_entry = tk.Entry(self.root)
        self.count_entry.grid(row=1, column=1, padx=5, pady=5)

        # Ping button
        self.ping_button = tk.Button(self.root, text="Ping", command=self.start_ping)
        self.ping_button.grid(row=2, column=0, columnspan=2, pady=10)

        # Results display
        self.results_text = tk.Text(self.root, height=15, width=50)
        self.results_text.grid(row=3, column=0, columnspan=2, padx=5, pady=5)

    def start_ping(self):
        '''
        Handles the ping operation when the user clicks the "Ping" button.
        '''
        host = self.host_entry.get()
        count = self.count_entry.get()

        if not host or not count.isdigit():
            messagebox.showerror("Error", "Please enter a valid host and count.")
            return

        count = int(count)
        self.results_text.delete(1.0, tk.END)

        # Perform the ping operation
        results = self.ping_tool.ping(host, count)
        self.display_results(results)

    def display_results(self, results):
        '''
        Displays the ping results in the GUI.

        :param results: The results of the ping operation.
        '''
        self.results_text.insert(tk.END, results)

    def run(self):
        '''
        Runs the GUI application.
        '''
        self.root.mainloop()
```

ping_tool.py
```python
'''
This file contains the PingTool class, which handles the logic for sending ICMP echo requests and calculating round-trip times.
'''
import subprocess
class PingTool:
    def __init__(self):
        pass
    def ping(self, host, count):
        '''
        Sends ICMP echo requests to the specified host and returns the results.
        :param host: The IP address or hostname to ping.
        :param count: The number of ICMP echo requests to send.
        :return: A tuple containing the raw output and parsed results.
        '''
        try:
            # Execute the ping command
            output = subprocess.check_output(
                ["ping", "-c", str(count), host],
                stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
                universal_newlines=True
            )
            return output
        except subprocess.CalledProcessError as e:
            return f"Error: {e.output}"
```

ping_gui.py
```python
'''
This file contains the PingGUI class, which manages the graphical user interface for the ping tool.
'''
import tkinter as tk
from tkinter import messagebox
from ping_tool import PingTool
class PingGUI:
    def __init__(self):
        '''
        Initializes the GUI components.
        '''
        self.ping_tool = PingTool()
        self.root = tk.Tk()
        self.root.title("Ping Tool")
        # Input fields
        self.host_label = tk.Label(self.root, text="Host:")
        self.host_label.grid(row=0, column=0, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.host_entry = tk.Entry(self.root)
        self.host_entry.grid(row=0, column=1, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.count_label = tk.Label(self.root, text="Count:")
        self.count_label.grid(row=1, column=0, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.count_entry = tk.Entry(self.root)
        self.count_entry.grid(row=1, column=1, padx=5, pady=5)
        # Ping button
        self.ping_button = tk.Button(self.root, text="Ping", command=self.start_ping)
        self.ping_button.grid(row=2, column=0, columnspan=2, pady=10)
        # Results display
        self.results_text = tk.Text(self.root, height=15, width=50)
        self.results_text.grid(row=3, column=0, columnspan=2, padx=5, pady=5)
    def start_ping(self):
        '''
        Handles the ping operation when the user clicks the "Ping" button.
        '''
        host = self.host_entry.get()
        count = self.count_entry.get()
        if not host or not count.isdigit():
            messagebox.showerror("Error", "Please enter a valid host and count.")
            return
        count = int(count)
        self.results_text.delete(1.0, tk.END)
        # Perform the ping operation
        results = self.ping_tool.ping(host, count)
        self.display_results(results)
    def display_results(self, results):
        '''
        Displays the ping results in the GUI.
        :param results: The results of the ping operation.
        '''
        self.results_text.insert(tk.END, results)
    def run(self):
        '''
        Runs the GUI application.
        '''
        self.root.mainloop()
```

Here is the fully implemented `ping_tool.py` file, strictly following the required format:

ping_tool.py
```python
'''
This file contains the PingTool class, which handles the logic for sending ICMP echo requests and calculating round-trip times.
'''
import subprocess
import re

class PingTool:
    def __init__(self):
        '''
        Initializes the PingTool class.
        '''
        pass

    def ping(self, host, count):
        '''
        Sends ICMP echo requests to the specified host and returns the results.
        :param host: The IP address or hostname to ping.
        :param count: The number of ICMP echo requests to send.
        :return: A string containing the parsed results of the ping operation.
        '''
        try:
            # Execute the ping command
            output = subprocess.check_output(
                ["ping", "-c", str(count), host],
                stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
                universal_newlines=True
            )
            # Parse the output
            parsed_results = self.parse_ping_output(output)
            return parsed_results
        except subprocess.CalledProcessError as e:
            return f"Error: {e.output}"
        except Exception as e:
            return f"An unexpected error occurred: {str(e)}"

    def parse_ping_output(self, output):
        '''
        Parses the raw output of the ping command to extract round-trip times and summary statistics.
        :param output: The raw output of the ping command.
        :return: A string containing the parsed results.
        '''
        try:
            lines = output.split("\n")
            results = []
            for line in lines:
                # Extract round-trip time from each ping response
                if "time=" in line:
                    match = re.search(r"time=(\d+\.\d+) ms", line)
                    if match:
                        results.append(f"Round-trip time: {match.group(1)} ms")
            # Extract summary statistics
            summary = [line for line in lines if "min/avg/max" in line]
            if summary:
                results.append("\nSummary:")
                results.append(summary[0])
            return "\n".join(results)
        except Exception as e:
            return f"Error parsing output: {str(e)}"
```

This implementation completes the `ping_tool.py` file by adding the `parse_ping_output` method to process the raw output of the ping command and extract meaningful information, such as round-trip times and summary statistics. The `ping` method now calls this parsing function to return user-friendly results.

```python
'''
This file contains the PingTool class, which handles the logic for sending ICMP echo requests and calculating round-trip times.
'''
import subprocess
import re

class PingTool:
    def __init__(self):
        '''
        Initializes the PingTool class.
        '''
        pass

    def ping(self, host, count):
        '''
        Sends ICMP echo requests to the specified host and returns the results.
        :param host: The IP address or hostname to ping.
        :param count: The number of ICMP echo requests to send.
        :return: A string containing the parsed results of the ping operation.
        '''
        try:
            # Execute the ping command
            output = subprocess.check_output(
                ["ping", "-c", str(count), host],
                stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
                universal_newlines=True
            )
            # Parse the output
            parsed_results = self.parse_ping_output(output)
            return parsed_results
        except subprocess.CalledProcessError as e:
            return f"Error: {e.output}"
        except Exception as e:
            return f"An unexpected error occurred: {str(e)}"

    def parse_ping_output(self, output):
        '''
        Parses the raw output of the ping command to extract round-trip times and summary statistics.
        :param output: The raw output of the ping command.
        :return: A string containing the parsed results.
        '''
        try:
            lines = output.split("\n")
            results = []
            for line in lines:
                # Extract round-trip time from each ping response
                if "time=" in line:
                    match = re.search(r"time=(\d+\.\d+) ms", line)
                    if match:
                        results.append(f"Round-trip time: {match.group(1)} ms")
            # Extract summary statistics
            summary = [line for line in lines if "min/avg/max" in line]
            if summary:
                results.append("\nSummary:")
                results.append(summary[0])
            return "\n".join(results)
        except Exception as e:
            return f"Error parsing output: {str(e)}"
```

import subprocess
import re
class PingTool:
    def __init__(self):
        '''
        Initializes the PingTool class.
        '''
        pass
    def ping(self, host, count):
        '''
        Sends ICMP echo requests to the specified host and returns the results.
        :param host: The IP address or hostname to ping.
        :param count: The number of ICMP echo requests to send.
        :return: A string containing the parsed results of the ping operation.
        '''
        try:
            # Execute the ping command
            output = subprocess.check_output(
                ["ping", "-c", str(count), host],
                stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
                universal_newlines=True
            )
            # Parse the output
            parsed_results = self.parse_ping_output(output)
            return parsed_results
        except subprocess.CalledProcessError as e:
            return f"Error: {e.output}"
        except Exception as e:
            return f"An unexpected error occurred: {str(e)}"
    def parse_ping_output(self, output):
        '''
        Parses the raw output of the ping command to extract round-trip times and summary statistics.
        :param output: The raw output of the ping command.
        :return: A string containing the parsed results.
        '''
        try:
            lines = output.split("\n")
            results = []
            for line in lines:
                # Extract round-trip time from each ping response
                if "time=" in line:
                    match = re.search(r"time=(\d+\.\d+) ms", line)
                    if match:
                        results.append(f"Round-trip time: {match.group(1)} ms")
            # Extract summary statistics
            summary = [line for line in lines if "min/avg/max" in line]
            if summary:
                results.append("\nSummary:")
                results.append(summary[0])
            return "\n".join(results)
        except Exception as e:
            return f"Error parsing output: {str(e)}"
```

ping_gui.py
```python
'''
This file contains the PingGUI class, which manages the graphical user interface for the ping tool.
'''
import tkinter as tk
from tkinter import messagebox
from ping_tool import PingTool
class PingGUI:
    def __init__(self):
        '''
        Initializes the GUI components.
        '''
        self.ping_tool = PingTool()
        self.root = tk.Tk()
        self.root.title("Ping Tool")
        # Input fields
        self.host_label = tk.Label(self.root, text="Host:")
        self.host_label.grid(row=0, column=0, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.host_entry = tk.Entry(self.root)
        self.host_entry.grid(row=0, column=1, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.count_label = tk.Label(self.root, text="Count:")
        self.count_label.grid(row=1, column=0, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.count_entry = tk.Entry(self.root)
        self.count_entry.grid(row=1, column=1, padx=5, pady=5)
        # Ping button
        self.ping_button = tk.Button(self.root, text="Ping", command=self.start_ping)
        self.ping_button.grid(row=2, column=0, columnspan=2, pady=10)
        # Results display
        self.results_text = tk.Text(self.root, height=15, width=50)
        self.results_text.grid(row=3, column=0, columnspan=2, padx=5, pady=5)
    def start_ping(self):
        '''
        Handles the ping operation when the user clicks the "Ping" button.
        '''
        host = self.host_entry.get()
        count = self.count_entry.get()
        if not host or not count.isdigit():
            messagebox.showerror("Error", "Please enter a valid host and count.")
            return
        count = int(count)
        self.results_text.delete(1.0, tk.END)
        # Perform the ping operation
        results = self.ping_tool.ping(host, count)
        self.display_results(results)
    def display_results(self, results):
        '''
        Displays the ping results in the GUI.
        :param results: The results of the ping operation.
        '''
        self.results_text.insert(tk.END, results)
    def run(self):
        '''
        Runs the GUI application.
        '''
        self.root.mainloop()
```

ping_tool.py  
```python  
'''  
This file contains the PingTool class, which handles the logic for sending ICMP echo requests and calculating round-trip times.  
'''  
import subprocess  
import re  

class PingTool:  
    def __init__(self):  
        '''  
        Initializes the PingTool class.  
        '''  
        pass  

    def ping(self, host, count):  
        '''  
        Sends ICMP echo requests to the specified host and returns the results.  
        :param host: The IP address or hostname to ping.  
        :param count: The number of ICMP echo requests to send.  
        :return: A string containing the parsed results of the ping operation.  
        '''  
        try:  
            # Execute the ping command  
            output = subprocess.check_output(  
                ["ping", "-c", str(count), host],  
                stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,  
                universal_newlines=True  
            )  
            # Parse the output  
            parsed_results = self.parse_ping_output(output)  
            return parsed_results  
        except subprocess.CalledProcessError as e:  
            return f"Error: {e.output}"  
        except Exception as e:  
            return f"An unexpected error occurred: {str(e)}"  

    def parse_ping_output(self, output):  
        '''  
        Parses the raw output of the ping command to extract round-trip times and summary statistics.  
        :param output: The raw output of the ping command.  
        :return: A string containing the parsed results.  
        '''  
        try:  
            lines = output.split("\n")  
            results = []  
            for line in lines:  
                # Extract round-trip time from each ping response  
                if "time=" in line:  
                    match = re.search(r"time=(\d+\.\d+) ms", line)  
                    if match:  
                        results.append(f"Round-trip time: {match.group(1)} ms")  
            # Extract summary statistics  
            summary = [line for line in lines if "min/avg/max" in line]  
            if summary:  
                results.append("\nSummary:")  
                results.append(summary[0])  
            return "\n".join(results)  
        except Exception as e:  
            return f"Error parsing output: {str(e)}"  
```

'''  
This file contains the PingTool class, which handles the logic for sending ICMP echo requests and calculating round-trip times.  
'''  
import subprocess  
import re  

class PingTool:  
    def __init__(self):  
        '''  
        Initializes the PingTool class.  
        '''  
        pass  

    def ping(self, host, count):  
        '''  
        Sends ICMP echo requests to the specified host and returns the results.  
        :param host: The IP address or hostname to ping.  
        :param count: The number of ICMP echo requests to send.  
        :return: A string containing the parsed results of the ping operation.  
        '''  
        try:  
            # Execute the ping command  
            output = subprocess.check_output(  
                ["ping", "-c", str(count), host],  
                stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,  
                universal_newlines=True  
            )  
            # Parse the output  
            parsed_results = self.parse_ping_output(output)  
            return parsed_results  
        except subprocess.CalledProcessError as e:  
            return f"Error: {e.output}"  
        except Exception as e:  
            return f"An unexpected error occurred: {str(e)}"  

    def parse_ping_output(self, output):  
        '''  
        Parses the raw output of the ping command to extract round-trip times and summary statistics.  
        :param output: The raw output of the ping command.  
        :return: A string containing the parsed results.  
        '''

            # Extract summary statistics  
            summary = [line for line in lines if "min/avg/max" in line]  
            if summary:  
                results.append("\nSummary:")  
                results.append(summary[0])  
            return "\n".join(results)

            return f"Error parsing output: {str(e)}"

ping_tool.py
```python
'''  
This file contains the PingTool class, which handles the logic for sending ICMP echo requests and calculating round-trip times.  
'''  
import subprocess  
import re  
class PingTool:  
    def __init__(self):  
        '''  
        Initializes the PingTool class.  
        '''  
        pass  
    def ping(self, host, count):  
        '''  
        Sends ICMP echo requests to the specified host and returns the results.  
        :param host: The IP address or hostname to ping.  
        :param count: The number of ICMP echo requests to send.  
        :return: A string containing the parsed results of the ping operation.  
        '''  
        try:  
            # Execute the ping command  
            output = subprocess.check_output(  
                ["ping", "-c", str(count), host],  
                stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,  
                universal_newlines=True  
            )  
            # Parse the output  
            parsed_results = self.parse_ping_output(output)  
            return parsed_results  
        except subprocess.CalledProcessError as e:  
            return f"Error: {e.output}"  
        except Exception as e:  
            return f"An unexpected error occurred: {str(e)}"  
    def parse_ping_output(self, output):  
        '''  
        Parses the raw output of the ping command to extract round-trip times and summary statistics.  
        :param output: The raw output of the ping command.  
        :return: A string containing the parsed results.  
        '''  
        try:  
            lines = output.split("\n")  
            results = []  
            for line in lines:  
                # Extract round-trip time from each ping response  
                if "time=" in line:  
                    match = re.search(r"time=(\d+\.\d+) ms", line)  
                    if match:  
                        results.append(f"Round-trip time: {match.group(1)} ms")  
            # Extract summary statistics  
            summary = [line for line in lines if "min/avg/max" in line]  
            if summary:  
                results.append("\nSummary:")  
                results.append(summary[0])  
            return "\n".join(results)  
        except Exception as e:  
            return f"Error parsing output: {str(e)}"  
```

ping_gui.py
```python
'''
This file contains the PingGUI class, which manages the graphical user interface for the ping tool.
'''
import tkinter as tk
from tkinter import messagebox
from ping_tool import PingTool
class PingGUI:
    def __init__(self):
        '''
        Initializes the GUI components.
        '''
        self.ping_tool = PingTool()
        self.root = tk.Tk()
        self.root.title("Ping Tool")
        # Input fields
        self.host_label = tk.Label(self.root, text="Host:")
        self.host_label.grid(row=0, column=0, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.host_entry = tk.Entry(self.root)
        self.host_entry.grid(row=0, column=1, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.count_label = tk.Label(self.root, text="Count:")
        self.count_label.grid(row=1, column=0, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.count_entry = tk.Entry(self.root)
        self.count_entry.grid(row=1, column=1, padx=5, pady=5)
        # Ping button
        self.ping_button = tk.Button(self.root, text="Ping", command=self.start_ping)
        self.ping_button.grid(row=2, column=0, columnspan=2, pady=10)
        # Results display
        self.results_text = tk.Text(self.root, height=15, width=50)
        self.results_text.grid(row=3, column=0, columnspan=2, padx=5, pady=5)
    def start_ping(self):
        '''
        Handles the ping operation when the user clicks the "Ping" button.
        '''
        host = self.host_entry.get()
        count = self.count_entry.get()
        if not host or not count.isdigit():
            messagebox.showerror("Error", "Please enter a valid host and count.")
            return
        count = int(count)
        self.results_text.delete(1.0, tk.END)
        # Perform the ping operation
        results = self.ping_tool.ping(host, count)
        self.display_results(results)
    def display_results(self, results):
        '''
        Displays the ping results in the GUI.
        :param results: The results of the ping operation.
        '''
        self.results_text.insert(tk.END, results)
    def run(self):
        '''
        Runs the GUI application.
        '''
        self.root.mainloop()
```

```python
'''
This file contains the CLIPingTool class, which provides a command-line interface for the ping tool.
'''
import argparse
from ping_tool import PingTool

class CLIPingTool:
    def __init__(self):
        '''
        Initializes the CLI Ping Tool and sets up the argument parser.
        '''
        self.ping_tool = PingTool()
        self.parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="CLI Network Ping Tool")
        self.parser.add_argument("host", type=str, help="The IP address or hostname to ping")
        self.parser.add_argument("count", type=int, help="The number of ICMP echo requests to send")

    def run(self):
        '''
        Parses the command-line arguments and performs the ping operation.
        '''
        args = self.parser.parse_args()
        host = args.host
        count = args.count
        try:
            results = self.ping_tool.ping(host, count)
            print(results)
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"An error occurred: {str(e)}")
```

'''

ping_tool.py
```python
'''  
This file contains the PingTool class, which handles the logic for sending ICMP echo requests and calculating round-trip times.  
'''  
import subprocess  
import re  
class PingTool:  
    def __init__(self):  
        '''  
        Initializes the PingTool class.  
        '''  
        pass  
    def ping(self, host, count):  
        '''  
        Sends ICMP echo requests to the specified host and returns the results.  
        :param host: The IP address or hostname to ping.  
        :param count: The number of ICMP echo requests to send.  
        :return: A string containing the parsed results of the ping operation.  
        '''  
        try:  
            # Execute the ping command  
            output = subprocess.check_output(  
                ["ping", "-c", str(count), host],  
                stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,  
                universal_newlines=True  
            )  
            # Parse the output  
            parsed_results = self.parse_ping_output(output)  
            return parsed_results  
        except subprocess.CalledProcessError as e:  
            return f"Error: {e.output}"  
        except Exception as e:  
            return f"An unexpected error occurred: {str(e)}"  
    def parse_ping_output(self, output):  
        '''  
        Parses the raw output of the ping command to extract round-trip times and summary statistics.  
        :param output: The raw output of the ping command.  
        :return: A string containing the parsed results.  
        '''  
        try:  
            lines = output.split("\n")  
            results = []  
            for line in lines:  
                # Extract round-trip time from each ping response  
                if "time=" in line:  
                    match = re.search(r"time=(\d+\.\d+) ms", line)  
                    if match:  
                        results.append(f"Round-trip time: {match.group(1)} ms")  
            # Extract summary statistics  
            summary = [line for line in lines if "min/avg/max" in line]  
            if summary:  
                results.append("\nSummary:")  
                results.append(summary[0])  
            return "\n".join(results)  
        except Exception as e:  
            return f"Error parsing output: {str(e)}"  
```

ping_gui.py
```python
'''
This file contains the PingGUI class, which manages the graphical user interface for the ping tool.
'''
import tkinter as tk
from tkinter import messagebox
from ping_tool import PingTool
class PingGUI:
    def __init__(self):
        '''
        Initializes the GUI components.
        '''
        self.ping_tool = PingTool()
        self.root = tk.Tk()
        self.root.title("Ping Tool")
        # Input fields
        self.host_label = tk.Label(self.root, text="Host:")
        self.host_label.grid(row=0, column=0, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.host_entry = tk.Entry(self.root)
        self.host_entry.grid(row=0, column=1, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.count_label = tk.Label(self.root, text="Count:")
        self.count_label.grid(row=1, column=0, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.count_entry = tk.Entry(self.root)
        self.count_entry.grid(row=1, column=1, padx=5, pady=5)
        # Ping button
        self.ping_button = tk.Button(self.root, text="Ping", command=self.start_ping)
        self.ping_button.grid(row=2, column=0, columnspan=2, pady=10)
        # Results display
        self.results_text = tk.Text(self.root, height=15, width=50)
        self.results_text.grid(row=3, column=0, columnspan=2, padx=5, pady=5)
    def start_ping(self):
        '''
        Handles the ping operation when the user clicks the "Ping" button.
        '''
        host = self.host_entry.get()
        count = self.count_entry.get()
        if not host or not count.isdigit():
            messagebox.showerror("Error", "Please enter a valid host and count.")
            return
        count = int(count)
        self.results_text.delete(1.0, tk.END)
        # Perform the ping operation
        results = self.ping_tool.ping(host, count)
        self.display_results(results)
    def display_results(self, results):
        '''
        Displays the ping results in the GUI.
        :param results: The results of the ping operation.
        '''
        self.results_text.insert(tk.END, results)
    def run(self):
        '''
        Runs the GUI application.
        '''
        self.root.mainloop()
```

cli_ping_tool.py
```python
'''
This file contains the CLIPingTool class, which provides a command-line interface for the ping tool.
'''
import argparse
from ping_tool import PingTool
class CLIPingTool:
    def __init__(self):
        '''
        Initializes the CLI Ping Tool and sets up the argument parser.
        '''
        self.ping_tool = PingTool()
        self.parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="CLI Network Ping Tool")
        self.parser.add_argument("host", type=str, help="The IP address or hostname to ping")
        self.parser.add_argument("count", type=int, help="The number of ICMP echo requests to send")
    def run(self):
        '''
        Parses the command-line arguments and performs the ping operation.
        '''
        args = self.parser.parse_args()
        host = args.host
        count = args.count
        try:
            results = self.ping_tool.ping(host, count)
            print(results)
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"An error occurred: {str(e)}")
```

ping_tool.py
```python
'''  
This file contains the PingTool class, which handles the logic for sending ICMP echo requests and calculating round-trip times.  
'''  
import subprocess  
import re  
class PingTool:  
    def __init__(self):  
        '''  
        Initializes the PingTool class.  
        '''  
        pass  
    def ping(self, host, count):  
        '''  
        Sends ICMP echo requests to the specified host and returns the results.  
        :param host: The IP address or hostname to ping.  
        :param count: The number of ICMP echo requests to send.  
        :return: A string containing the parsed results of the ping operation.  
        '''  
        try:  
            # Execute the ping command  
            output = subprocess.check_output(  
                ["ping", "-c", str(count), host],  
                stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,  
                universal_newlines=True  
            )  
            # Parse the output  
            parsed_results = self.parse_ping_output(output)  
            return parsed_results  
        except subprocess.CalledProcessError as e:  
            return f"Error: {e.output}"  
        except Exception as e:  
            return f"An unexpected error occurred: {str(e)}"  
    def parse_ping_output(self, output):  
        '''  
        Parses the raw output of the ping command to extract round-trip times and summary statistics.  
        :param output: The raw output of the ping command.  
        :return: A string containing the parsed results.  
        '''  
        try:  
            lines = output.split("\n")  
            results = []  
            for line in lines:  
                # Extract round-trip time from each ping response  
                if "time=" in line:  
                    match = re.search(r"time=(\d+\.\d+) ms", line)  
                    if match:  
                        results.append(f"Round-trip time: {match.group(1)} ms")  
            # Extract summary statistics  
            summary = [line for line in lines if "min/avg/max" in line]  
            if summary:  
                results.append("\nSummary:")  
                results.append(summary[0])  
            return "\n".join(results)  
        except Exception as e:  
            return f"Error parsing output: {str(e)}"  
```

ping_tool.py
```python
'''
This file contains the PingTool class, which handles the logic for sending ICMP echo requests and calculating round-trip times.
'''
import subprocess
import re

class PingTool:
    def __init__(self):
        '''
        Initializes the PingTool class.
        '''
        pass

    def ping(self, host, count):
        '''
        Sends ICMP echo requests to the specified host and returns the results.
        :param host: The IP address or hostname to ping.
        :param count: The number of ICMP echo requests to send.
        :return: A string containing the parsed results of the ping operation.
        '''
        try:
            # Execute the ping command
            output = subprocess.check_output(
                ["ping", "-c", str(count), host],
                stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
                universal_newlines=True
            )
            # Parse the output
            parsed_results = self.parse_ping_output(output)
            return parsed_results
        except subprocess.CalledProcessError as e:
            return f"Error: {e.output}"
        except Exception as e:
            return f"An unexpected error occurred: {str(e)}"

    def parse_ping_output(self, output):
        '''
        Parses the raw output of the ping command to extract round-trip times and summary statistics.
        :param output: The raw output of the ping command.
        :return: A string containing the parsed results.
        '''
        try:
            lines = output.split("\n")
            results = []
            for line in lines:
                # Extract round-trip time from each ping response
                if "time=" in line:
                    match = re.search(r"time=(\d+\.\d+) ms", line)
                    if match:
                        results.append(f"Round-trip time: {match.group(1)} ms")
            # Extract summary statistics
            summary = [line for line in lines if "min/avg/max" in line]
            if summary:
                results.append("\nSummary:")
                results.append(summary[0])
            return "\n".join(results)
        except Exception as e:
            return f"Error parsing output: {str(e)}"
```

ping_gui.py
```python
'''
This file contains the PingGUI class, which manages the graphical user interface for the ping tool.
'''
import tkinter as tk
from tkinter import messagebox
from ping_tool import PingTool

class PingGUI:
    def __init__(self):
        '''
        Initializes the GUI components.
        '''
        self.ping_tool = PingTool()
        self.root = tk.Tk()
        self.root.title("Ping Tool")
        # Input fields
        self.host_label = tk.Label(self.root, text="Host:")
        self.host_label.grid(row=0, column=0, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.host_entry = tk.Entry(self.root)
        self.host_entry.grid(row=0, column=1, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.count_label = tk.Label(self.root, text="Count:")
        self.count_label.grid(row=1, column=0, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.count_entry = tk.Entry(self.root)
        self.count_entry.grid(row=1, column=1, padx=5, pady=5)
        # Ping button
        self.ping_button = tk.Button(self.root, text="Ping", command=self.start_ping)
        self.ping_button.grid(row=2, column=0, columnspan=2, pady=10)
        # Results display
        self.results_text = tk.Text(self.root, height=15, width=50)
        self.results_text.grid(row=3, column=0, columnspan=2, padx=5, pady=5)

    def start_ping(self):
        '''
        Handles the ping operation when the user clicks the "Ping" button.
        '''
        host = self.host_entry.get()
        count = self.count_entry.get()
        if not host or not count.isdigit():
            messagebox.showerror("Error", "Please enter a valid host and count.")
            return
        count = int(count)
        self.results_text.delete(1.0, tk.END)
        # Perform the ping operation
        results = self.ping_tool.ping(host, count)
        self.display_results(results)

    def display_results(self, results):
        '''
        Displays the ping results in the GUI.
        :param results: The results of the ping operation.
        '''
        self.results_text.insert(tk.END, results)

    def run(self):
        '''
        Runs the GUI application.
        '''
        self.root.mainloop()
```

cli_ping_tool.py
```python
'''
This file contains the CLIPingTool class, which provides a command-line interface for the ping tool.
'''
import argparse
from ping_tool import PingTool

class CLIPingTool:
    def __init__(self):
        '''
        Initializes the CLI Ping Tool and sets up the argument parser.
        '''
        self.ping_tool = PingTool()
        self.parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="CLI Network Ping Tool")
        self.parser.add_argument("host", type=str, help="The IP address or hostname to ping")
        self.parser.add_argument("count", type=int, help="The number of ICMP echo requests to send")

    def run(self):
        '''
        Parses the command-line arguments and performs the ping operation.
        '''
        args = self.parser.parse_args()
        host = args.host
        count = args.count
        try:
            results = self.ping_tool.ping(host, count)
            print(results)
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"An error occurred: {str(e)}")

ping_tool.py
```python
'''
This file contains the PingTool class, which handles the logic for sending ICMP echo requests and calculating round-trip times.
'''
import subprocess
import re

class PingTool:
    def __init__(self):
        '''
        Initializes the PingTool class.
        '''
        pass

    def ping(self, host, count):
        '''
        Sends ICMP echo requests to the specified host and returns the results.
        :param host: The IP address or hostname to ping.
        :param count: The number of ICMP echo requests to send.
        :return: A string containing the parsed results of the ping operation.
        '''
        try:
            # Execute the ping command
            output = subprocess.check_output(
                ["ping", "-c", str(count), host],
                stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
                universal_newlines=True
            )
            # Parse the output
            parsed_results = self.parse_ping_output(output)
            return parsed_results
        except subprocess.CalledProcessError as e:
            return f"Error: {e.output}"
        except Exception as e:
            return f"An unexpected error occurred: {str(e)}"

    def parse_ping_output(self, output):
        '''
        Parses the raw output of the ping command to extract round-trip times and summary statistics.
        :param output: The raw output of the ping command.
        :return: A string containing the parsed results.
        '''
        try:
            lines = output.split("\n")
            results = []
            for line in lines:
                # Extract round-trip time from each ping response
                if "time=" in line:
                    match = re.search(r"time=(\d+\.\d+) ms", line)
                    if match:
                        results.append(f"Round-trip time: {match.group(1)} ms")
            # Extract summary statistics
            summary = [line for line in lines if "min/avg/max" in line]
            if summary:
                results.append("\nSummary:")
                results.append(summary[0])
            return "\n".join(results)
        except Exception as e:
            return f"Error parsing output: {str(e)}"
```

ping_tool.py
```python
'''
This file contains the PingTool class, which handles the logic for sending ICMP echo requests and calculating round-trip times.
'''
import subprocess
import re
class PingTool:
    def __init__(self):
        '''
        Initializes the PingTool class.
        '''
        pass
    def ping(self, host, count):
        '''
        Sends ICMP echo requests to the specified host and returns the results.
        :param host: The IP address or hostname to ping.
        :param count: The number of ICMP echo requests to send.
        :return: A string containing the parsed results of the ping operation.
        '''
        try:
            # Execute the ping command
            output = subprocess.check_output(
                ["ping", "-c", str(count), host],
                stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
                universal_newlines=True
            )
            # Parse the output
            parsed_results = self.parse_ping_output(output)
            return parsed_results
        except subprocess.CalledProcessError as e:
            return f"Error: {e.output}"
        except Exception as e:
            return f"An unexpected error occurred: {str(e)}"
    def parse_ping_output(self, output):
        '''
        Parses the raw output of the ping command to extract round-trip times and summary statistics.
        :param output: The raw output of the ping command.
        :return: A string containing the parsed results.
        '''
        try:
            lines = output.split("\n")
            results = []
            for line in lines:
                # Extract round-trip time from each ping response
                if "time=" in line:
                    match = re.search(r"time=(\d+\.\d+) ms", line)
                    if match:
                        results.append(f"Round-trip time: {match.group(1)} ms")
            # Extract summary statistics
            summary = [line for line in lines if "min/avg/max" in line]
            if summary:
                results.append("\nSummary:")
                results.append(summary[0])
            return "\n".join(results)
        except Exception as e:
            return f"Error parsing output: {str(e)}"
```

ping_gui.py
```python
'''
This file contains the PingGUI class, which manages the graphical user interface for the ping tool.
'''
import tkinter as tk
from tkinter import messagebox
from ping_tool import PingTool
class PingGUI:
    def __init__(self):
        '''
        Initializes the GUI components.
        '''
        self.ping_tool = PingTool()
        self.root = tk.Tk()
        self.root.title("Ping Tool")
        # Input fields
        self.host_label = tk.Label(self.root, text="Host:")
        self.host_label.grid(row=0, column=0, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.host_entry = tk.Entry(self.root)
        self.host_entry.grid(row=0, column=1, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.count_label = tk.Label(self.root, text="Count:")
        self.count_label.grid(row=1, column=0, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.count_entry = tk.Entry(self.root)
        self.count_entry.grid(row=1, column=1, padx=5, pady=5)
        # Ping button
        self.ping_button = tk.Button(self.root, text="Ping", command=self.start_ping)
        self.ping_button.grid(row=2, column=0, columnspan=2, pady=10)
        # Results display
        self.results_text = tk.Text(self.root, height=15, width=50)
        self.results_text.grid(row=3, column=0, columnspan=2, padx=5, pady=5)
    def start_ping(self):
        '''
        Handles the ping operation when the user clicks the "Ping" button.
        '''
        host = self.host_entry.get()
        count = self.count_entry.get()
        if not host or not count.isdigit():
            messagebox.showerror("Error", "Please enter a valid host and count.")
            return
        count = int(count)
        self.results_text.delete(1.0, tk.END)
        # Perform the ping operation
        results = self.ping_tool.ping(host, count)
        self.display_results(results)
    def display_results(self, results):
        '''
        Displays the ping results in the GUI.
        :param results: The results of the ping operation.
        '''
        self.results_text.insert(tk.END, results)
    def run(self):
        '''
        Runs the GUI application.
        '''
        self.root.mainloop()
```

cli_ping_tool.py
```python
'''
This file contains the CLIPingTool class, which provides a command-line interface for the ping tool.
'''
import argparse
from ping_tool import PingTool
class CLIPingTool:
    def __init__(self):
        '''
        Initializes the CLI Ping Tool and sets up the argument parser.
        '''
        self.ping_tool = PingTool()
        self.parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="CLI Network Ping Tool")
        self.parser.add_argument("host", type=str, help="The IP address or hostname to ping")
        self.parser.add_argument("count", type=int, help="The number of ICMP echo requests to send")
    def run(self):
        '''
        Parses the command-line arguments and performs the ping operation.
        '''
        args = self.parser.parse_args()
        host = args.host
        count = args.count
        try:
            results = self.ping_tool.ping(host, count)
            print(results)
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"An error occurred: {str(e)}")

ping_tool.py
```python
'''
This file contains the PingTool class, which handles the logic for sending ICMP echo requests and calculating round-trip times.
'''
import subprocess
import re
class PingTool:
    def __init__(self):
        '''
        Initializes the PingTool class.
        '''
        pass
    def ping(self, host, count):
        '''
        Sends ICMP echo requests to the specified host and returns the results.
        :param host: The IP address or hostname to ping.
        :param count: The number of ICMP echo requests to send.
        :return: A string containing the parsed results of the ping operation.
        '''
        try:
            # Execute the ping command
            output = subprocess.check_output(
                ["ping", "-c", str(count), host],
                stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
                universal_newlines=True
            )
            # Parse the output
            parsed_results = self.parse_ping_output(output)
            return parsed_results
        except subprocess.CalledProcessError as e:
            return f"Error: {e.output}"
        except Exception as e:
            return f"An unexpected error occurred: {str(e)}"
    def parse_ping_output(self, output):
        '''
        Parses the raw output of the ping command to extract round-trip times and summary statistics.
        :param output: The raw output of the ping command.
        :return: A string containing the parsed results.
        '''
        try:
            lines = output.split("\n")
            results = []
            for line in lines:
                # Extract round-trip time from each ping response
                if "time=" in line:
                    match = re.search(r"time=(\d+\.\d+) ms", line)
                    if match:
                        results.append(f"Round-trip time: {match.group(1)} ms")
            # Extract summary statistics
            summary = [line for line in lines if "min/avg/max" in line]
            if summary:
                results.append("\nSummary:")
                results.append(summary[0])
            return "\n".join(results)
        except Exception as e:
            return f"Error parsing output: {str(e)}"
```

ping_gui.py
```python
'''
This file contains the PingGUI class, which manages the graphical user interface for the ping tool.
'''
import tkinter as tk
from tkinter import messagebox
from ping_tool import PingTool
class PingGUI:
    def __init__(self):
        '''
        Initializes the GUI components.
        '''
        self.ping_tool = PingTool()
        self.root = tk.Tk()
        self.root.title("Ping Tool")
        # Input fields
        self.host_label = tk.Label(self.root, text="Host:")
        self.host_label.grid(row=0, column=0, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.host_entry = tk.Entry(self.root)
        self.host_entry.grid(row=0, column=1, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.count_label = tk.Label(self.root, text="Count:")
        self.count_label.grid(row=1, column=0, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.count_entry = tk.Entry(self.root)
        self.count_entry.grid(row=1, column=1, padx=5, pady=5)
        # Ping button
        self.ping_button = tk.Button(self.root, text="Ping", command=self.start_ping)
        self.ping_button.grid(row=2, column=0, columnspan=2, pady=10)
        # Results display
        self.results_text = tk.Text(self.root, height=15, width=50)
        self.results_text.grid(row=3, column=0, columnspan=2, padx=5, pady=5)
    def start_ping(self):
        '''
        Handles the ping operation when the user clicks the "Ping" button.
        '''
        host = self.host_entry.get()
        count = self.count_entry.get()
        if not host or not count.isdigit():
            messagebox.showerror("Error", "Please enter a valid host and count.")
            return
        count = int(count)
        self.results_text.delete(1.0, tk.END)
        # Perform the ping operation
        results = self.ping_tool.ping(host, count)
        self.display_results(results)
    def display_results(self, results):
        '''
        Displays the ping results in the GUI.
        :param results: The results of the ping operation.
        '''
        self.results_text.insert(tk.END, results)
    def run(self):
        '''
        Runs the GUI application.
        '''
        self.root.mainloop()
```

cli_ping_tool.py
```python
'''
This file contains the CLIPingTool class, which provides a command-line interface for the ping tool.
'''
import argparse
from ping_tool import PingTool
class CLIPingTool:
    def __init__(self):
        '''
        Initializes the CLI Ping Tool and sets up the argument parser.
        '''
        self.ping_tool = PingTool()
        self.parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="CLI Network Ping Tool")
        self.parser.add_argument("host", type=str, help="The IP address or hostname to ping")
        self.parser.add_argument("count", type=int, help="The number of ICMP echo requests to send")
    def run(self):
        '''
        Parses the command-line arguments and performs the ping operation.
        '''
        args = self.parser.parse_args()
        host = args.host
        count = args.count
        try:
            results = self.ping_tool.ping(host, count)
            print(results)
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"An error occurred: {str(e)}")
```

ping_tool.py
```python
'''
This file contains the PingTool class, which handles the logic for sending ICMP echo requests and calculating round-trip times.
'''
import subprocess
import re

class PingTool:
    def __init__(self):
        '''
        Initializes the PingTool class.
        '''
        pass

    def ping(self, host, count):
        '''
        Sends ICMP echo requests to the specified host and returns the results.
        :param host: The IP address or hostname to ping.
        :param count: The number of ICMP echo requests to send.
        :return: A string containing the parsed results of the ping operation.
        '''
        try:
            # Execute the ping command
            output = subprocess.check_output(
                ["ping", "-c", str(count), host],
                stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
                universal_newlines=True
            )
            # Parse the output
            parsed_results = self.parse_ping_output(output)
            return parsed_results
        except subprocess.CalledProcessError as e:
            return f"Error: {e.output}"
        except Exception as e:
            return f"An unexpected error occurred: {str(e)}"

    def parse_ping_output(self, output):
        '''
        Parses the raw output of the ping command to extract round-trip times and summary statistics.
        :param output: The raw output of the ping command.
        :return: A string containing the parsed results.
        '''
        try:
            lines = output.split("\n")
            results = []
            for line in lines:
                # Extract round-trip time from each ping response
                if "time=" in line:
                    match = re.search(r"time=(\d+\.\d+) ms", line)
                    if match:
                        results.append(f"Round-trip time: {match.group(1)} ms")
            # Extract summary statistics
            summary = [line for line in lines if "min/avg/max" in line]
            if summary:
                results.append("\nSummary:")
                results.append(summary[0])
            return "\n".join(results)
        except Exception as e:
            return f"Error parsing output: {str(e)}"
```

ping_gui.py
```python
'''
This file contains the PingGUI class, which manages the graphical user interface for the ping tool.
'''
import tkinter as tk
from tkinter import messagebox
from ping_tool import PingTool

class PingGUI:
    def __init__(self):
        '''
        Initializes the GUI components.
        '''
        self.ping_tool = PingTool()
        self.root = tk.Tk()
        self.root.title("Ping Tool")
        # Input fields
        self.host_label = tk.Label(self.root, text="Host:")
        self.host_label.grid(row=0, column=0, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.host_entry = tk.Entry(self.root)
        self.host_entry.grid(row=0, column=1, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.count_label = tk.Label(self.root, text="Count:")
        self.count_label.grid(row=1, column=0, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.count_entry = tk.Entry(self.root)
        self.count_entry.grid(row=1, column=1, padx=5, pady=5)
        # Ping button
        self.ping_button = tk.Button(self.root, text="Ping", command=self.start_ping)
        self.ping_button.grid(row=2, column=0, columnspan=2, pady=10)
        # Results display
        self.results_text = tk.Text(self.root, height=15, width=50)
        self.results_text.grid(row=3, column=0, columnspan=2, padx=5, pady=5)

    def start_ping(self):
        '''
        Handles the ping operation when the user clicks the "Ping" button.
        '''
        host = self.host_entry.get()
        count = self.count_entry.get()
        if not host or not count.isdigit():
            messagebox.showerror("Error", "Please enter a valid host and count.")
            return
        count = int(count)
        self.results_text.delete(1.0, tk.END)
        # Perform the ping operation
        results = self.ping_tool.ping(host, count)
        self.display_results(results)

    def display_results(self, results):
        '''
        Displays the ping results in the GUI.
        :param results: The results of the ping operation.
        '''
        self.results_text.insert(tk.END, results)

    def run(self):
        '''
        Runs the GUI application.
        '''
        self.root.mainloop()
```

cli_ping_tool.py

'''
This file contains the CLIPingTool class, which provides a command-line interface for the ping tool.
'''
import argparse
from ping_tool import PingTool

class CLIPingTool:
    def __init__(self):
        '''
        Initializes the CLI Ping Tool and sets up the argument parser.
        '''
        self.ping_tool = PingTool()
        self.parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="CLI Network Ping Tool")
        self.parser.add_argument("host", type=str, help="The IP address or hostname to ping")
        self.parser.add_argument("count", type=int, help="The number of ICMP echo requests to send")

    def run(self):
        '''
        Parses the command-line arguments and performs the ping operation.
        '''
        args = self.parser.parse_args()
        host = args.host
        count = args.count
        try:
            results = self.ping_tool.ping(host, count)
            print(results)
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"An error occurred: {str(e)}")

import re

class PingTool:
    def __init__(self):
        '''
        Initializes the PingTool class.
        '''
        pass

    def ping(self, host, count):

        Sends ICMP echo requests to the specified host and returns the results.
        :param host: The IP address or hostname to ping.

                stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
                universal_newlines=True
            )

            return parsed_results

            return f"Error: {e.output}"
        except Exception as e:
            return f"An unexpected error occurred: {str(e)}"

    def parse_ping_output(self, output):
        '''
        Parses the raw output of the ping command to extract round-trip times and summary statistics.
        :param output: The raw output of the ping command.
        :return: A string containing the parsed results.

        Displays the ping results in the GUI.
        :param results: The results of the ping operation.
        '''
        self.results_text.insert(tk.END, results)

    def run(self):
        '''
        Runs the GUI application.
        '''
        self.root.mainloop()
```

cli_ping_tool.py
```python
'''
This file contains the CLIPingTool class, which provides a command-line interface for the ping tool.
'''
import argparse
from ping_tool import PingTool

class CLIPingTool:
    def __init__(self):
        '''
        Initializes the CLI Ping Tool and sets up the argument parser.
        '''
        self.ping_tool = PingTool()
        self.parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="CLI Network Ping Tool")
        self.parser.add_argument("host", type=str, help="The IP address or hostname to ping")
        self.parser.add_argument("count", type=int, help="The number of ICMP echo requests to send")

    def run(self):
        '''
        Parses the command-line arguments and performs the ping operation.
        '''
        args = self.parser.parse_args()
        host = args.host
        count = args.count
        try:
            results = self.ping_tool.ping(host, count)
            print(results)
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"An error occurred: {str(e)}")

ping_tool.py
```python
'''
This file contains the PingTool class, which handles the logic for sending ICMP echo requests and calculating round-trip times.
'''
import subprocess
import re
class PingTool:
    def __init__(self):
        '''
        Initializes the PingTool class.
        '''
        pass
    def ping(self, host, count):
        '''
        Sends ICMP echo requests to the specified host and returns the results.
        :param host: The IP address or hostname to ping.
        :param count: The number of ICMP echo requests to send.
        :return: A string containing the parsed results of the ping operation.
        '''
        try:
            # Execute the ping command
            output = subprocess.check_output(
                ["ping", "-c", str(count), host],
                stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
                universal_newlines=True
            )
            # Parse the output
            parsed_results = self.parse_ping_output(output)
            return parsed_results
        except subprocess.CalledProcessError as e:
            return f"Error: {e.output}"
        except Exception as e:
            return f"An unexpected error occurred: {str(e)}"
    def parse_ping_output(self, output):
        '''
        Parses the raw output of the ping command to extract round-trip times and summary statistics.
        :param output: The raw output of the ping command.
        :return: A string containing the parsed results.
        '''
        try:
            lines = output.split("\n")
            results = []
            for line in lines:
                # Extract round-trip time from each ping response
                if "time=" in line:
                    match = re.search(r"time=(\d+\.\d+) ms", line)
                    if match:
                        results.append(f"Round-trip time: {match.group(1)} ms")
            # Extract summary statistics
            summary = [line for line in lines if "min/avg/max" in line]
            if summary:
                results.append("\nSummary:")
                results.append(summary[0])
            return "\n".join(results)
        except Exception as e:
            return f"Error parsing output: {str(e)}"
```

ping_gui.py
```python
'''
This file contains the PingGUI class, which manages the graphical user interface for the ping tool.
'''
import tkinter as tk
from tkinter import messagebox
from ping_tool import PingTool
class PingGUI:
    def __init__(self):
        '''
        Initializes the GUI components.
        '''
        self.ping_tool = PingTool()
        self.root = tk.Tk()
        self.root.title("Ping Tool")
        # Input fields
        self.host_label = tk.Label(self.root, text="Host:")
        self.host_label.grid(row=0, column=0, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.host_entry = tk.Entry(self.root)
        self.host_entry.grid(row=0, column=1, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.count_label = tk.Label(self.root, text="Count:")
        self.count_label.grid(row=1, column=0, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.count_entry = tk.Entry(self.root)
        self.count_entry.grid(row=1, column=1, padx=5, pady=5)
        # Ping button
        self.ping_button = tk.Button(self.root, text="Ping", command=self.start_ping)
        self.ping_button.grid(row=2, column=0, columnspan=2, pady=10)
        # Results display
        self.results_text = tk.Text(self.root, height=15, width=50)
        self.results_text.grid(row=3, column=0, columnspan=2, padx=5, pady=5)
    def start_ping(self):
        '''
        Handles the ping operation when the user clicks the "Ping" button.
        '''
        host = self.host_entry.get()
        count = self.count_entry.get()
        if not host or not count.isdigit():
            messagebox.showerror("Error", "Please enter a valid host and count.")
            return
        count = int(count)
        self.results_text.delete(1.0, tk.END)
        # Perform the ping operation
        results = self.ping_tool.ping(host, count)
        self.display_results(results)
    def display_results(self, results):
        '''
        Displays the ping results in the GUI.
        :param results: The results of the ping operation.
        '''
        self.results_text.insert(tk.END, results)
    def run(self):
        '''
        Runs the GUI application.
        '''
        self.root.mainloop()
```

cli_ping_tool.py
```python
'''
This file contains the CLIPingTool class, which provides a command-line interface for the ping tool.
'''
import argparse
from ping_tool import PingTool
class CLIPingTool:
    def __init__(self):
        '''
        Initializes the CLI Ping Tool and sets up the argument parser.
        '''
        self.ping_tool = PingTool()
        self.parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="CLI Network Ping Tool")
        self.parser.add_argument("host", type=str, help="The IP address or hostname to ping")
        self.parser.add_argument("count", type=int, help="The number of ICMP echo requests to send")
    def run(self):
        '''
        Parses the command-line arguments and performs the ping operation.
        '''
        args = self.parser.parse_args()
        host = args.host
        count = args.count
        try:
            results = self.ping_tool.ping(host, count)
            print(results)
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"An error occurred: {str(e)}")

ping_tool.py
```python
'''
This file contains the PingTool class, which handles the logic for sending ICMP echo requests and calculating round-trip times.
'''
import subprocess
import re
class PingTool:
    def __init__(self):
        '''
        Initializes the PingTool class.
        '''
        pass
    def ping(self, host, count):
        '''
        Sends ICMP echo requests to the specified host and returns the results.
        :param host: The IP address or hostname to ping.
        :param count: The number of ICMP echo requests to send.
        :return: A string containing the parsed results of the ping operation.
        '''
        try:
            # Execute the ping command
            output = subprocess.check_output(
                ["ping", "-c", str(count), host],
                stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
                universal_newlines=True
            )
            # Parse the output
            parsed_results = self.parse_ping_output(output)
            return parsed_results
        except subprocess.CalledProcessError as e:
            return f"Error: {e.output}"
        except Exception as e:
            return f"An unexpected error occurred: {str(e)}"
    def parse_ping_output(self, output):
        '''
        Parses the raw output of the ping command to extract round-trip times and summary statistics.
        :param output: The raw output of the ping command.
        :return: A string containing the parsed results.
        '''
        try:
            lines = output.split("\n")
            results = []
            for line in lines:
                # Extract round-trip time from each ping response
                if "time=" in line:
                    match = re.search(r"time=(\d+\.\d+) ms", line)
                    if match:
                        results.append(f"Round-trip time: {match.group(1)} ms")
            # Extract summary statistics
            summary = [line for line in lines if "min/avg/max" in line]
            if summary:
                results.append("\nSummary:")
                results.append(summary[0])
            return "\n".join(results)
        except Exception as e:
            return f"Error parsing output: {str(e)}"
```

ping_gui.py
```python
'''
This file contains the PingGUI class, which manages the graphical user interface for the ping tool.
'''
import tkinter as tk
from tkinter import messagebox
from ping_tool import PingTool
class PingGUI:
    def __init__(self):
        '''
        Initializes the GUI components.
        '''
        self.ping_tool = PingTool()
        self.root = tk.Tk()
        self.root.title("Ping Tool")
        # Input fields
        self.host_label = tk.Label(self.root, text="Host:")
        self.host_label.grid(row=0, column=0, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.host_entry = tk.Entry(self.root)
        self.host_entry.grid(row=0, column=1, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.count_label = tk.Label(self.root, text="Count:")
        self.count_label.grid(row=1, column=0, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.count_entry = tk.Entry(self.root)
        self.count_entry.grid(row=1, column=1, padx=5, pady=5)
        # Ping button
        self.ping_button = tk.Button(self.root, text="Ping", command=self.start_ping)
        self.ping_button.grid(row=2, column=0, columnspan=2, pady=10)
        # Results display
        self.results_text = tk.Text(self.root, height=15, width=50)
        self.results_text.grid(row=3, column=0, columnspan=2, padx=5, pady=5)
    def start_ping(self):
        '''
        Handles the ping operation when the user clicks the "Ping" button.
        '''
        host = self.host_entry.get()
        count = self.count_entry.get()
        if not host or not count.isdigit():
            messagebox.showerror("Error", "Please enter a valid host and count.")
            return
        count = int(count)
        self.results_text.delete(1.0, tk.END)
        # Perform the ping operation
        results = self.ping_tool.ping(host, count)
        self.display_results(results)
    def display_results(self, results):
        '''
        Displays the ping results in the GUI.
        :param results: The results of the ping operation.
        '''
        self.results_text.insert(tk.END, results)
    def run(self):
        '''
        Runs the GUI application.
        '''
        self.root.mainloop()
```

cli_ping_tool.py
```python
'''
This file contains the CLIPingTool class, which provides a command-line interface for the ping tool.
'''
import argparse
from ping_tool import PingTool
class CLIPingTool:
    def __init__(self):
        '''
        Initializes the CLI Ping Tool and sets up the argument parser.
        '''
        self.ping_tool = PingTool()
        self.parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="CLI Network Ping Tool")
        self.parser.add_argument("host", type=str, help="The IP address or hostname to ping")
        self.parser.add_argument("count", type=int, help="The number of ICMP echo requests to send")
    def run(self):
        '''
        Parses the command-line arguments and performs the ping operation.
        '''
        args = self.parser.parse_args()
        host = args.host
        count = args.count
        try:
            results = self.ping_tool.ping(host, count)
            print(results)
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"An error occurred: {str(e)}")
```

'''
This file contains the PingTool class, which handles the logic for sending ICMP echo requests and calculating round-trip times.
'''
import subprocess
import re

class PingTool:
    def __init__(self):
        '''
        Initializes the PingTool class.
        '''
        pass

    def ping(self, host, count):
        '''
        Sends ICMP echo requests to the specified host and returns the results.
        :param host: The IP address or hostname to ping.
        :param count: The number of ICMP echo requests to send.
        :return: A string containing the parsed results of the ping operation.
        '''
        try:
            # Execute the ping command
            output = subprocess.check_output(
                ["ping", "-c", str(count), host],
                stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
                universal_newlines=True
            )
            # Parse the output
            parsed_results = self.parse_ping_output(output)
            return parsed_results
        except subprocess.CalledProcessError as e:
            return f"Error: {e.output}"
        except Exception as e:
            return f"An unexpected error occurred: {str(e)}"

    def parse_ping_output(self, output):
        '''
        Parses the raw output of the ping command to extract round-trip times and summary statistics.
        :param output: The raw output of the ping command.
        :return: A string containing the parsed results.
        '''
        try:
            lines = output.split("\n")
            results = []
            for line in lines:
                # Extract round-trip time from each ping response
                if "time=" in line:
                    match = re.search(r"time=(\d+\.\d+) ms", line)
                    if match:
                        results.append(f"Round-trip time: {match.group(1)} ms")
            # Extract summary statistics
            summary = [line for line in lines if "min/avg/max" in line]
            if summary:
                results.append("\nSummary:")
                results.append(summary[0])
            return "\n".join(results)
        except Exception as e:
            return f"Error parsing output: {str(e)}"
```

ping_gui.py
```python
'''
This file contains the PingGUI class, which manages the graphical user interface for the ping tool.
'''
import tkinter as tk
from tkinter import messagebox
from ping_tool import PingTool

class PingGUI:
    def __init__(self):
        '''
        Initializes the GUI components.
        '''
        self.ping_tool = PingTool()
        self.root = tk.Tk()
        self.root.title("Ping Tool")
        # Input fields
        self.host_label = tk.Label(self.root, text="Host:")
        self.host_label.grid(row=0, column=0, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.host_entry = tk.Entry(self.root)
        self.host_entry.grid(row=0, column=1, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.count_label = tk.Label(self.root, text="Count:")
        self.count_label.grid(row=1, column=0, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.count_entry = tk.Entry(self.root)
        self.count_entry.grid(row=1, column=1, padx=5, pady=5)
        # Ping button
        self.ping_button = tk.Button(self.root, text="Ping", command=self.start_ping)
        self.ping_button.grid(row=2, column=0, columnspan=2, pady=10)
        # Results display
        self.results_text = tk.Text(self.root, height=15, width=50)
        self.results_text.grid(row=3, column=0, columnspan=2, padx=5, pady=5)

    def start_ping(self):
        '''
        Handles the ping operation when the user clicks the "Ping" button.
        '''
        host = self.host_entry.get()
        count = self.count_entry.get()
        if not host or not count.isdigit():
            messagebox.showerror("Error", "Please enter a valid host and count.")
            return
        count = int(count)
        self.results_text.delete(1.0, tk.END)
        # Perform the ping operation
        results = self.ping_tool.ping(host, count)
        self.display_results(results)

    def display_results(self, results):
        '''
        Displays the ping results in the GUI.
        :param results: The results of the ping operation.
        '''
        self.results_text.insert(tk.END, results)

    def run(self):
        '''
        Runs the GUI application.
        '''
        self.root.mainloop()
```

cli_ping_tool.py
```python
'''
This file contains the CLIPingTool class, which provides a command-line interface for the ping tool.
'''
import argparse
from ping_tool import PingTool

class CLIPingTool:
    def __init__(self):
        '''
        Initializes the CLI Ping Tool and sets up the argument parser.
        '''
        self.ping_tool = PingTool()
        self.parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="CLI Network Ping Tool")
        self.parser.add_argument("host", type=str, help="The IP address or hostname to ping")
        self.parser.add_argument("count", type=int, help="The number of ICMP echo requests to send")

    def run(self):
        '''
        Parses the command-line arguments and performs the ping operation.
        '''
        args = self.parser.parse_args()
        host = args.host
        count = args.count
        try:
            results = self.ping_tool.ping(host, count)
            print(results)
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"An error occurred: {str(e)}")
