ping_tool.py
```python
'''
This file contains the PingTool class, which handles the logic for sending ICMP echo requests and calculating round-trip times.
'''
import subprocess
import re

class PingTool:
    def __init__(self):
        '''
        Initializes the PingTool class.
        '''
        pass

    def ping(self, host, count):
        '''
        Sends ICMP echo requests to the specified host and returns the results.
        :param host: The IP address or hostname to ping.
        :param count: The number of ICMP echo requests to send.
        :return: A string containing the parsed results of the ping operation.
        '''
        try:
            # Execute the ping command
            output = subprocess.check_output(
                ["ping", "-c", str(count), host],
                stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
                universal_newlines=True
            )
            # Parse the output
            parsed_results = self.parse_ping_output(output)
            return parsed_results
        except subprocess.CalledProcessError as e:
            return f"Error: {e.output}"
        except Exception as e:
            return f"An unexpected error occurred: {str(e)}"

    def parse_ping_output(self, output):
        '''
        Parses the raw output of the ping command to extract round-trip times and summary statistics.
        :param output: The raw output of the ping command.
        :return: A string containing the parsed results.
        '''
        try:
            lines = output.split("\n")
            results = []
            for line in lines:
                # Extract round-trip time from each ping response
                if "time=" in line:
                    match = re.search(r"time=(\d+\.\d+) ms", line)
                    if match:
                        results.append(f"Round-trip time: {match.group(1)} ms")
            # Extract summary statistics
            summary = [line for line in lines if "min/avg/max" in line]
            if summary:
                results.append("\nSummary:")
                results.append(summary[0])
            return "\n".join(results)
        except Exception as e:
            return f"Error parsing output: {str(e)}"

```python
'''
This file contains the PingTool class, which handles the logic for sending ICMP echo requests and calculating round-trip times.
'''
import subprocess
import re
class PingTool:
    def __init__(self):
        '''
        Initializes the PingTool class.
        '''
        pass
    def ping(self, host, count):
        '''
        Sends ICMP echo requests to the specified host and returns the results.
        :param host: The IP address or hostname to ping.
        :param count: The number of ICMP echo requests to send.
        :return: A string containing the parsed results of the ping operation.
        '''
        try:
            # Execute the ping command
            output = subprocess.check_output(
                ["ping", "-c", str(count), host],
                stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
                universal_newlines=True
            )
            # Parse the output
            parsed_results = self.parse_ping_output(output)
            return parsed_results
        except subprocess.CalledProcessError as e:
            return f"Error: {e.output}"
        except Exception as e:
            return f"An unexpected error occurred: {str(e)}"
    def parse_ping_output(self, output):
        '''
        Parses the raw output of the ping command to extract round-trip times and summary statistics.
        :param output: The raw output of the ping command.
        :return: A string containing the parsed results.
        '''
        try:
            lines = output.split("\n")
            results = []
            for line in lines:
                # Extract round-trip time from each ping response
                if "time=" in line:
                    match = re.search(r"time=(\d+\.\d+) ms", line)
                    if match:
                        results.append(f"Round-trip time: {match.group(1)} ms")
            # Extract summary statistics
            summary = [line for line in lines if "min/avg/max" in line]
            if summary:
                results.append("\nSummary:")
                results.append(summary[0])
            return "\n".join(results)
        except Exception as e:
            return f"Error parsing output: {str(e)}"

        Initializes the GUI components.

        # Input fields
        self.host_label = tk.Label(self.root, text="Host:")

        self.host_entry = tk.Entry(self.root)
        self.host_entry.grid(row=0, column=1, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.count_label = tk.Label(self.root, text="Count:")
        self.count_label.grid(row=1, column=0, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.count_entry = tk.Entry(self.root)
        self.count_entry.grid(row=1, column=1, padx=5, pady=5)
        # Ping button
        self.ping_button = tk.Button(self.root, text="Ping", command=self.start_ping)
        self.ping_button.grid(row=2, column=0, columnspan=2, pady=10)
        # Results display
        self.results_text = tk.Text(self.root, height=15, width=50)
        self.results_text.grid(row=3, column=0, columnspan=2, padx=5, pady=5)
    def start_ping(self):
        '''
        Handles the ping operation when the user clicks the "Ping" button.
        '''
        host = self.host_entry.get()
        count = self.count_entry.get()
        if not host or not count.isdigit():
            messagebox.showerror("Error", "Please enter a valid host and count.")
            return
        count = int(count)
        self.results_text.delete(1.0, tk.END)
        # Perform the ping operation
        results = self.ping_tool.ping(host, count)
        self.display_results(results)
    def display_results(self, results):
        '''
        Displays the ping results in the GUI.
        :param results: The results of the ping operation.
        '''
        self.results_text.insert(tk.END, results)
    def run(self):
        '''
        Runs the GUI application.
        '''
        self.root.mainloop()
```

cli_ping_tool.py
```python
'''
This file contains the CLIPingTool class, which provides a command-line interface for the ping tool.
'''
import argparse
from ping_tool import PingTool
class CLIPingTool:
    def __init__(self):
        '''
        Initializes the CLI Ping Tool and sets up the argument parser.
        '''
        self.ping_tool = PingTool()
        self.parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="CLI Network Ping Tool")
        self.parser.add_argument("host", type=str, help="The IP address or hostname to ping")
        self.parser.add_argument("count", type=int, help="The number of ICMP echo requests to send")
    def run(self):
        '''
        Parses the command-line arguments and performs the ping operation.
        '''
        args = self.parser.parse_args()
        host = args.host
        count = args.count
        try:
            results = self.ping_tool.ping(host, count)
            print(results)
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"An error occurred: {str(e)}")
```

ping_tool.py
```python
'''
This file contains the PingTool class, which handles the logic for sending ICMP echo requests and calculating round-trip times.
'''
import subprocess
import re
class PingTool:
    def __init__(self):
        '''
        Initializes the PingTool class.
        '''
        pass
    def ping(self, host, count):
        '''
        Sends ICMP echo requests to the specified host and returns the results.
        :param host: The IP address or hostname to ping.
        :param count: The number of ICMP echo requests to send.
        :return: A string containing the parsed results of the ping operation.
        '''
        try:
            # Execute the ping command
            output = subprocess.check_output(
                ["ping", "-c", str(count), host],
                stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
                universal_newlines=True
            )
            # Parse the output
            parsed_results = self.parse_ping_output(output)
            return parsed_results
        except subprocess.CalledProcessError as e:
            return f"Error: {e.output}"
        except Exception as e:
            return f"An unexpected error occurred: {str(e)}"
    def parse_ping_output(self, output):
        '''
        Parses the raw output of the ping command to extract round-trip times and summary statistics.
        :param output: The raw output of the ping command.
        :return: A string containing the parsed results.
        '''
        try:
            lines = output.split("\n")
            results = []
            for line in lines:
                # Extract round-trip time from each ping response
                if "time=" in line:
                    match = re.search(r"time=(\d+\.\d+) ms", line)
                    if match:
                        results.append(f"Round-trip time: {match.group(1)} ms")
            # Extract summary statistics
            summary = [line for line in lines if "min/avg/max" in line]
            if summary:
                results.append("\nSummary:")
                results.append(summary[0])
            return "\n".join(results)
        except Exception as e:
            return f"Error parsing output: {str(e)}"
```

```python
'''
This file contains the PingGUI class, which manages the graphical user interface for the ping tool.
'''
import tkinter as tk
from tkinter import messagebox
from ping_tool import PingTool
class PingGUI:
    def __init__(self):
        '''
        Initializes the GUI components.
        '''
        self.ping_tool = PingTool()
        self.root = tk.Tk()
        self.root.title("Ping Tool")
        # Input fields
        self.host_label = tk.Label(self.root, text="Host:")
        self.host_label.grid(row=0, column=0, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.host_entry = tk.Entry(self.root)
        self.host_entry.grid(row=0, column=1, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.count_label = tk.Label(self.root, text="Count:")
        self.count_label.grid(row=1, column=0, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.count_entry = tk.Entry(self.root)
        self.count_entry.grid(row=1, column=1, padx=5, pady=5)
        # Ping button
        self.ping_button = tk.Button(self.root, text="Ping", command=self.start_ping)
        self.ping_button.grid(row=2, column=0, columnspan=2, pady=10)
        # Results display
        self.results_text = tk.Text(self.root, height=15, width=50)
        self.results_text.grid(row=3, column=0, columnspan=2, padx=5, pady=5)
    def start_ping(self):
        '''
        Handles the ping operation when the user clicks the "Ping" button.
        '''
        host = self.host_entry.get()
        count = self.count_entry.get()
        if not host or not count.isdigit():
            messagebox.showerror("Error", "Please enter a valid host and count.")
            return
        count = int(count)
        self.results_text.delete(1.0, tk.END)
        # Perform the ping operation
        results = self.ping_tool.ping(host, count)
        self.display_results(results)
    def display_results(self, results):
        '''
        Displays the ping results in the GUI.
        :param results: The results of the ping operation.
        '''
        self.results_text.insert(tk.END, results)
    def run(self):
        '''
        Runs the GUI application.
        '''
        self.root.mainloop()

ping_tool.py
```python
'''
This file contains the PingTool class, which handles the logic for sending ICMP echo requests and calculating round-trip times.
'''
import subprocess
import re
class PingTool:
    def __init__(self):
        '''
        Initializes the PingTool class.
        '''
        pass
    def ping(self, host, count):
        '''
        Sends ICMP echo requests to the specified host and returns the results.
        :param host: The IP address or hostname to ping.
        :param count: The number of ICMP echo requests to send.
        :return: A string containing the parsed results of the ping operation.
        '''
        try:
            # Execute the ping command
            output = subprocess.check_output(
                ["ping", "-c", str(count), host],
                stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
                universal_newlines=True
            )
            # Parse the output
            parsed_results = self.parse_ping_output(output)
            return parsed_results
        except subprocess.CalledProcessError as e:
            return f"Error: {e.output}"
        except Exception as e:
            return f"An unexpected error occurred: {str(e)}"
    def parse_ping_output(self, output):
        '''
        Parses the raw output of the ping command to extract round-trip times and summary statistics.
        :param output: The raw output of the ping command.
        :return: A string containing the parsed results.
        '''
        try:
            lines = output.split("\n")
            results = []
            for line in lines:
                # Extract round-trip time from each ping response
                if "time=" in line:
                    match = re.search(r"time=(\d+\.\d+) ms", line)
                    if match:
                        results.append(f"Round-trip time: {match.group(1)} ms")
            # Extract summary statistics
            summary = [line for line in lines if "min/avg/max" in line]
            if summary:
                results.append("\nSummary:")
                results.append(summary[0])
            return "\n".join(results)
        except Exception as e:
            return f"Error parsing output: {str(e)}"
```

ping_gui.py
```python
'''
This file contains the PingGUI class, which manages the graphical user interface for the ping tool.
'''
import tkinter as tk
from tkinter import messagebox
from ping_tool import PingTool
class PingGUI:
    def __init__(self):
        '''
        Initializes the GUI components.
        '''
        self.ping_tool = PingTool()
        self.root = tk.Tk()
        self.root.title("Ping Tool")
        # Input fields
        self.host_label = tk.Label(self.root, text="Host:")
        self.host_label.grid(row=0, column=0, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.host_entry = tk.Entry(self.root)
        self.host_entry.grid(row=0, column=1, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.count_label = tk.Label(self.root, text="Count:")
        self.count_label.grid(row=1, column=0, padx=5, pady=5)
        self.count_entry = tk.Entry(self.root)
        self.count_entry.grid(row=1, column=1, padx=5, pady=5)
        # Ping button
        self.ping_button = tk.Button(self.root, text="Ping", command=self.start_ping)
        self.ping_button.grid(row=2, column=0, columnspan=2, pady=10)
        # Results display
        self.results_text = tk.Text(self.root, height=15, width=50)
        self.results_text.grid(row=3, column=0, columnspan=2, padx=5, pady=5)
    def start_ping(self):
        '''
        Handles the ping operation when the user clicks the "Ping" button.
        '''
        host = self.host_entry.get()
        count = self.count_entry.get()
        if not host or not count.isdigit():
            messagebox.showerror("Error", "Please enter a valid host and count.")
            return
        count = int(count)
        self.results_text.delete(1.0, tk.END)
        # Perform the ping operation
        results = self.ping_tool.ping(host, count)
        self.display_results(results)
    def display_results(self, results):
        '''
        Displays the ping results in the GUI.
        :param results: The results of the ping operation.
        '''
        self.results_text.insert(tk.END, results)
    def run(self):
        '''
        Runs the GUI application.
        '''
        self.root.mainloop()
```

cli_ping_tool.py
```python
'''
This file contains the CLIPingTool class, which provides a command-line interface for the ping tool.
'''
import argparse
from ping_tool import PingTool
class CLIPingTool:
    def __init__(self):
        '''
        Initializes the CLI Ping Tool and sets up the argument parser.
        '''
        self.ping_tool = PingTool()
        self.parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="CLI Network Ping Tool")
        self.parser.add_argument("host", type=str, help="The IP address or hostname to ping")
        self.parser.add_argument("count", type=int, help="The number of ICMP echo requests to send")
    def run(self):
        '''
        Parses the command-line arguments and performs the ping operation.
        '''
        args = self.parser.parse_args()
        host = args.host
        count = args.count
        try:
            results = self.ping_tool.ping(host, count)
            print(results)
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"An error occurred: {str(e)}")
```

```markdown
# CLI Network Ping Tool

A simple yet powerful tool to send ICMP echo requests to a specified IP address or hostname. The tool provides both a Command-Line Interface (CLI) and a Graphical User Interface (GUI) for user convenience.

---

## 📖 Overview

The CLI Network Ping Tool allows users to:
- Send ICMP echo requests to a specified IP address or hostname.
- Configure the number of ICMP echo requests to send.
- View round-trip times for each request.
- Display a summary of the ping operation, including minimum, average, and maximum round-trip times.

The tool is implemented in Python and provides two modes of operation:
1. **Command-Line Interface (CLI)**: For users who prefer terminal-based interaction.
2. **Graphical User Interface (GUI)**: For users who prefer a visual interface.

---

## 🚀 Quick Install

### Prerequisites
Ensure you have Python 3.6 or later installed on your system. You can download Python from [python.org](https://www.python.org/).

### Installation Steps
1. Clone the repository or download the source code.
   ```bash
   git clone https://github.com/your-repo/cli-network-ping-tool.git
   cd cli-network-ping-tool
   ```
2. Install the required dependencies using `pip`.
   ```bash
   pip install -r requirements.txt
   ```
   If the `requirements.txt` file is not provided, ensure you have the following Python libraries installed:
   - `tkinter` (for GUI)
   - `argparse` (for CLI)
   - `subprocess` (for executing ping commands)
   - `re` (for parsing ping output)

---

## 🤔 How to Use

### 1. Command-Line Interface (CLI)
The CLI mode allows you to interact with the tool directly from the terminal.

#### Usage
Run the `cli_ping_tool.py` script with the following arguments:
- `host`: The IP address or hostname to ping.
- `count`: The number of ICMP echo requests to send.

#### Example
```bash
python cli_ping_tool.py google.com 5
```

#### Output
The tool will display the round-trip time for each request and a summary of the ping operation.

---

### 2. Graphical User Interface (GUI)
The GUI mode provides a user-friendly interface for performing ping operations.

#### Usage
Run the `main.py` script to launch the GUI application.
```bash
python main.py
```

#### Steps
1. Enter the IP address or hostname in the "Host" field.
2. Enter the number of ICMP echo requests in the "Count" field.
3. Click the "Ping" button to start the operation.
4. View the results in the text area below.

#### Example
- Host: `google.com`
- Count: `5`

The results will display the round-trip time for each request and a summary of the operation.

---

## 🛠️ Features

### CLI Features
- Lightweight and fast.
- Ideal for advanced users who prefer terminal-based tools.

### GUI Features
- User-friendly interface.
- Suitable for users who prefer visual interaction.
- Displays results in a clear and organized manner.

---

## ⚠️ Notes and Limitations
- The tool uses the `ping` command, which may require administrative privileges on some systems.
- Ensure that the target host is reachable and allows ICMP echo requests.
- The tool is designed for educational and diagnostic purposes only.

---

## 📞 Support
For any issues or feature requests, please contact our support team or raise an issue on our GitHub repository.

---

## 📜 License
This project is licensed under the MIT License. See the LICENSE file for details.

---

Thank you for using the CLI Network Ping Tool! 🚀
```

 ```markdown
# CLI Network Ping Tool

A simple yet powerful tool to send ICMP echo requests to a specified IP address or hostname. The tool provides both a Command-Line Interface (CLI) and a Graphical User Interface (GUI) for user convenience.

---

2. **Graphical User Interface (GUI)**: For users who prefer a visual interface.

---

## 🚀 Quick Install

### Prerequisites

### Installation Steps
1. Clone the repository or download the source code.
   ```bash
   git clone https://github.com/your-repo/cli-network-ping-tool.git
   cd cli-network-ping-tool
   ```
2. Install the required dependencies using `pip`.
   ```bash
   pip install -r requirements.txt
   ```
   If the `requirements.txt` file is not provided, ensure you have the following Python libraries installed:
   - `tkinter` (for GUI)
   - `argparse` (for CLI)
   - `subprocess` (for executing ping commands)
   - `re` (for parsing ping output)

---

## 🤔 How to Use

### 1. Command-Line Interface (CLI)
The CLI mode allows you to interact with the tool directly from the terminal.

#### Usage
Run the `cli_ping_tool.py` script with the following arguments:
- `host`: The IP address or hostname to ping.
- `count`: The number of ICMP echo requests to send.

#### Example
```bash
python cli_ping_tool.py google.com 5
```

#### Output
The tool will display the round-trip time for each request and a summary of the ping operation.

---

### 2. Graphical User Interface (GUI)
The GUI mode provides a user-friendly interface for performing ping operations.

#### Usage
Run the `main.py` script to launch the GUI application.
```bash
python main.py
```

#### Steps
1. Enter the IP address or hostname in the "Host" field.
2. Enter the number of ICMP echo requests in the "Count" field.
3. Click the "Ping" button to start the operation.
4. View the results in the text area below.

#### Example
- Host: `google.com`
- Count: `5`

The results will display the round-trip time for each request and a summary of the operation.

---

## 🛠️ Features

### CLI Features
- Lightweight and fast.
- Ideal for advanced users who prefer terminal-based tools.

### GUI Features
- User-friendly interface.
- Suitable for users who prefer visual interaction.
- Displays results in a clear and organized manner.

---

## ⚠️ Notes and Limitations
- The tool uses the `ping` command, which may require administrative privileges on some systems.
- Ensure that the target host is reachable and allows ICMP echo requests.
- The tool is designed for educational and diagnostic purposes only.
